# Proposed OEIS comments: A208841 to A218061

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A208841

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A208841>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8726](../papers/8501-9000/8726-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +7*a(n-2) -6*a(n-3).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 81 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A209507>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8165](../papers/8001-8500/8165-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 16*a(n-1) - 65*a(n-2) + 92*a(n-3) - 48*a(n-4) + 8*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 3*x*(81 - 443*x + 692*x^2 - 376*x^3 + 64*x^4) / (1 - 16*x + 65*x^2 - 92*x^3 + 48*x^4 - 8*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 10 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A209533

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A209533>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3302](../papers/3001-3500/3302-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 16^(n+1)+1

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical closed form is correct. The count is a walk count in a finite graph, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; the conjectured form satisfies the same one, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to agree from then on, for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A209538

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A209538>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [1515](../papers/1501-2000/1515-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) +a(n-2) -72*a(n-5) +288*a(n-6) +72*a(n-7) +306*a(n-10) -1224*a(n-11) -306*a(n-12) +72*a(n-15) -288*a(n-16) -72*a(n-17) -a(n-20) +4*a(n-21) +a(n-22).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 15 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 72 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 21. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A209603

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A209603>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [2675](../papers/2501-3000/2675-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 - x + x^2 - x^3 + 2*x^4) / ((1 - x + x^3)*(1 - x - x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 11 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition ranges over the whole array but reaches only as far as the value itself, which the alphabet bounds, so carrying that many lines together with one bit per cell -- has this cell found its partner yet -- makes the count a walk count on 8 states. Hence a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A210064

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A210064>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9591](../papers/9501-10000/9591-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(n-3)*a(n) +2*(-4*n^2+11*n-2)*a(n-1) +8*(n-1)*(2*n-3)*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 08 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree -1, so the recurrence holds for n > -1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A210781

Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [1141](../papers/1001-1500/1141-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-2) +2*a(n-3) +4*a(n-4) +38*a(n-5) +106*a(n-6) +172*a(n-7) +596*a(n-8) +573*a(n-9) +971*a(n-10) +744*a(n-11) -1425*a(n-12) -4335*a(n-13) -10525*a(n-14) -11517*a(n-15) -13462*a(n-16) -6001*a(n-17) -4772*a(n-18) +619*a(n-19) +10548*a(n-20) +45667*a(n-21) +64053*a(n-22) +34800*a(n-23) -11095*a(n-24) -26829*a(n-25) +41629*a(n-26) +81493*a(n-27) -35792*a(n-28) -237880*a(n-29) -186310*a(n-30) +61561*a(n-31) +67920*a(n-32) -147561*a(n-33) -221267*a(n-34) -90437*a(n-35) -13145*a(n-36) +33145*a(n-37) +101632*a(n-38) +63235*a(n-39) +3056*a(n-40) -2932*a(n-41) -6086*a(n-42) -5505*a(n-43) +115*a(n-44) +649*a(n-45) +319*a(n-46) +258*a(n-47) +4*a(n-48) -24*a(n-49) for n>51.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition ranges over the whole array but reaches only as far as the value itself, which the alphabet bounds, so carrying that many lines together with one bit per cell -- has this cell found its partner yet -- makes the count a walk count on 99 states. Hence a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 51. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [9156](../papers/9001-9500/9156-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-3).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jul 18 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A211491

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A211491>  
Result obtained: **4 September 2026**  
Paper: [103](../papers/0001-0500/0103-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - 21*a(n-3) + 16*a(n-4) + 29*a(n-5) - 34*a(n-6) - 6*a(n-7) + 12*a(n-8).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(14 - 28*x - 71*x^2 + 149*x^3 + 93*x^4 - 222*x^5 - 19*x^6 + 82*x^7) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - x - x^2)*(1 - 2*x^2)*(1 - 3*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 84 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A212205

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A212205>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9292](../papers/9001-9500/9292-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: (n+1)*a(n) +2*(n-1)*a(n-1) +6*(-2*n+1)*a(n-2) +12*(-2*n+5)*a(n-3) +(37*n-77)*a(n-4) +2*(37*n-151)*a(n-5) +6*(-n+5)*a(n-6) +12*(-n+7)*a(n-7)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Aug 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 6, so the recurrence holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A212925

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A212925>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [3299](../papers/3001-3500/3299-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 37*a(n-1) -279*a(n-2) +243*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(5 + 99*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 9*x)*(1 - 27*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Jul 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. "In all rows" quantifies over the whole array, so no bounded window decides it, but it is a conjunction of independent per-row facts: keep one bit per adjacent column pair, recording whether every row so far has put column j above column j-1. A row can only clear bits, and the array is admissible exactly when all are clear at the end. So the state is that bit mask alone, giving 3 states, however large the alphabet, and a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A213203

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A213203>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [9013](../papers/9001-9500/9013-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: a(n) + (-n^2-n-11)*a(n-1) + (n^3+7*n^2-13*n+39)*a(n-2) - 2*(n-2)*(4*n^2-2*n-15)*a(n-3) + 20*(n-2)*(n-3)*(n-4)*a(n-4) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 21 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A215973

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A215973>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9401](../papers/9001-9500/9401-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) +3*(-3*n+1)*a(n-1) +4*(5*n-7)*a(n-2) +2*(-8*n+19)*a(n-3) +2*(2*n-7)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A217358

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A217358>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [9696](../papers/9501-10000/9696-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 46*n*(n-1)*(n-2)*a(n) -(n-1)*(n-2)*(11*n-74)*a(n-1) -(n-2)*(336*n^2-1359*n+1351)*a(n-2) +(-347*n^3+2190*n^2-3861*n+1330)*a(n-3) + 8*(2*n-7)*(4*n-15)*(4*n-17)*a(n-4) = 0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [983](../papers/0501-1000/0983-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 - 3*x + 3*x^2 - x^3) / (1 - 2*x - x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 19 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 12 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A217711

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A217711>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9385](../papers/9001-9500/9385-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -(n+1)*(25*n-3314)*a(n) -5*n*(5*n+9446)*a(n-1) +2*(594*n^2 +128863*n -142613)*a(n-2) +16*(-119*n^2-39230*n+87888)*a(n-3) -32*(2*n-7)*(53*n-8687)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 08 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
